\subsection{Manuscript~06: exhaustive preorder tests}
The program enumerates every reflexive relation on a labeled $n$-element
set and retains exactly those that are transitive. For each resulting
preorder it independently enumerates all nonnegative integer vectors of
total at most $n$ and tests every ideal inequality. It also enumerates
ordered disjoint support pairs and decides matchability by recursive
bipartite matching, without using the ideal Hall criterion in that matching
routine. The resulting counts are compared in the following ways:
\begin{enumerate}[itemsep=2pt,topsep=4pt]
\item Each individual support fibre is compared with the Boolean-interval
count in \eqref{gam:eq:fixedsupport}; this tests the multivariate identity,
not only a specialization.
\item Gamma expansion, the quotient-block formula, and both sharp upper
bounds are checked. Matchability is also compared with the ideal Hall test.
\item All equal-size support pairs in the doubled graph are tested for
common-support cancellation, and its polynomial is compared with $h_\tau$.
An independent undirected matching recursion checks
$\deg\Gamma_\tau=\nu(\Comp(\tau))$.
\end{enumerate}

\begin{table}[htbp]
\centering
\caption{Manuscript~06's recorded exhaustive run. A support fibre is
specified by a preorder and a subset of its labeled ground set.}
\label{gam:tab:checks}
\begin{tabular}{@{}rrrr@{}}
\toprule
$n$ & Preorders & Support fibres & Equal-size doubled pairs\\
\midrule
0 & 1 & 1 & 1\\
1 & 1 & 2 & 2\\
2 & 4 & 16 & 24\\
3 & 29 & 232 & 580\\
4 & 355 & 5,680 & 24,850\\
5 & 6,942 & 222,144 & 1,749,384\\
\midrule
Total & 7,332 & 228,075 & 1,774,841\\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{table}
All comparisons passed. These are labeled preorders, including nontrivial
equivalence classes; the totals do not count only unlabeled posets.
The accompanying CSV contains every tested preorder and both coefficient
vectors, not merely summary totals.

\emph{Additional independent scopes.} The demand/support identity, including
its prescribed-support refinement, was tested on all 689 bipartite graphs
with each labeled shore of size at most three, including empty shores. The
run counts 5,880 feasible demand vectors across those graphs.

The full transitivity/cancellation/palindromicity characterization and both
endpoint coefficient formulas were tested on all 4,166 reflexive relations
through size four. This includes nontransitive relations, so it tests the
negative direction rather than assuming the input is a preorder. [Merge
note: $4{,}166$ counts the empty relation at $n=0$; there are $4{,}165$ with
$1\le n\le4$.]

A further 140 distinct deterministic samples have sizes six, seven, and
eight: respectively 46, 47, and 47 examples. They were generated with seed
\texttt{20260929} by taking transitive closures of both arbitrary directed
relations and acyclic ones. This is not uniform sampling from preorders.
Every sampled relation is listed in the JSON output. The direct support
counts, gamma formula, block formula, ideal Hall criterion, extremal bounds,
and matching-number identity were checked on these samples. Exhaustive
all-pairs doubled cancellation was reserved for the small-size run.

Finally, the binomial and Narayana specializations were compared with their
gamma expansions through $n=20$. These last tests are identities between
explicit formulas, not exhaustive lattice-point enumerations in dimension
20. No root approximations, floating-point certificates, or randomized
claims of proof are used.

\emph{Files and execution.} The delivered command, run from the package
root, was \texttt{python code/verify.py --max-n 5 --out data}. In this
directory, pass a new output directory, so that the shipped record is
preserved (the default \texttt{--out data} is relative to the working
directory and would write unprefixed files):
\begin{quote}\small\ttfamily
py code/05-gamma-positivity-verify.py --max-n 5 --out /path/to/scratch
\end{quote}
Python 3.10 or later is required. No external Python packages are needed.
The program refuses to run under \texttt{python -O}, because it uses
assertions. It deliberately rejects exhaustive sizes above five, whose
relation search grows too rapidly for its intended role. The article's
production algorithm can instead enumerate signed pairs and use an
augmenting-path matching test, requiring at most $O(3^n n^3)$ elementary
operations and polynomial workspace; the verifier uses simple recursive
matching because its small-instance independence is more important than
asymptotic speed.

The package also contains \texttt{README.md}, \texttt{SOURCE\_AUDIT.md},
\texttt{PROOF\_STATUS.md}, a build script, and the compiled PDF. The theorem
proofs are in this article, not in the test log. [Merge note: the two notes
and the build script are shipped under the prefixed names above; the
delivery README and PDF are not shipped.]

\subsection{Manuscript~01: independent representations and completed suites}
Manuscript~01's program performs several logically distinct finite checks
rather than evaluating the same formula twice. The direct enumerator lists
all nonnegative $n$-vectors with total at most $n$, computes every lower
ideal from the input relation, and tests the original
inequalities~\eqref{gam:eq:Q}. It records the number of lattice points
\emph{for each exact support}. This part does not use Hall's condition,
matching supports, or a gamma expansion.

A separate recursive matching checker tests bijections between selected
source and receiver masks. It does not use the ideal inequalities or the
Hall inequalities. Disjoint compatible pairs are accumulated to predict
both the gamma coefficients and every multivariate coefficient in
\eqref{gam:eq:multi}. Independently, compatible pairs with overlapping sets
are accumulated to check \eqref{gam:eq:doubled}. The program also checks
cancellation of overlaps, complement-duality, the maximum-matching degree
law, and the deletion--differentiation identity using directly enumerated
deleted preorders.

Finally, for fixed bipartite shores, the program enumerates integer demands
by their defining subset inequalities and compares their exact support
counts against matching-support counts. This directly tests the classical
bridge and its support refinement on thousands of separate graphs.

Every reflexive relation on a fixed labeled $n$-element set is generated,
and exactly the transitive ones are retained. Thus the following is an
exhaustive test of \emph{labeled preorders}, not merely of naturally labeled
posets and not merely of random closures:
\begin{center}
\begin{tabular}{@{}rrrrrrr@{}}
\toprule
$n$ & 0 & 1 & 2 & 3 & 4 & 5\\
\midrule
Preorders checked & 1 & 1 & 4 & 29 & 355 & 6,942\\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
All 7,332 cases passed. Additional completed checks comprise all 512
bipartite graphs on fixed shores of sizes $(3,3)$, all 4,096 on shores
$(3,4)$, all 64 on shores $(2,3)$, and the empty-shore boundary cases.
A seeded generation of 120 preorder closures on six or seven elements
produced 119 distinct relations, all of which passed. These latter cases
are explicitly \emph{not} exhaustive.

The block formula was checked on 87 cases: every preorder on three
indexed blocks, with block-size vectors $(1,1,1)$, $(2,1,2)$, and $(2,2,2)$.
Allowing equivalent block indices is harmless for the capacity formula,
although the canonical equivalence-class quotient in
Theorem~\ref{gam:gv:thm:blocks} is a poset. The five named examples below are also
included in the saved output.

\midrule

To reproduce the full finite run, manuscript~01 gave, from the extracted
package directory, \texttt{python3 code/verify.py --max-n 5 --output
results/verification.json}. In this directory pass an explicit output path
(the default is relative to the working directory):
\begin{quote}\small\ttfamily
py code/04-gamma-vector-verify.py --max-n 5 \textbackslash\\
\hspace*{2em}--output /path/to/scratch/verification.json
\end{quote}
Python 3.10 or later is required. No external Python packages, network
access, computer algebra system, or proof-assistant installation is used.
The JSON records the interpreter version, random seed, case counts,
examples, and elapsed times. The recorded times are properties of this run,
not performance guarantees.

\emph{What the checks establish (manuscript~01).} Exhaustive agreement
detects normalization mistakes, reversed relations, incorrect treatment of
equivalence classes, confusion between matchings and supports, and
implementation errors in small cases. It does not prove an all-cardinality
assertion. The infinite statements are justified by the arguments in
Sections~\ref{gam:sec:count}--\ref{gam:sec:statistics}, with the classical
inputs \eqref{htwo:eq:KPinput}--\eqref{htwo:eq:OTinput} explicitly imported.

and $\Theta_3$ identities by hand; all agree. These scratch checks are not

\section{Proof dependencies, audit, and a formalization route}\label{gam:sec:audit}
The mathematical dependency chain for the main theorem is short
(manuscript~06):
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 \text{classical augmented-root/hypertree identities}\\
 \Downarrow\\
 \text{bipartite demand/support equinumeracy}\\
 \Downarrow\\
 \text{preorder support-fibre equivalence and finite Boolean summation}\\
 \Downarrow\\
 \text{the full gamma and multivariate formulas.}
 \end{gathered}
\]
The other principal chain is independent:
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 \text{finite matching, reachability, and transitivity}\\
 \Downarrow\\
 [t]p_R=|R|,\quad[t^{n-1}]p_R=|\overline R|,\quad
 \text{common-support cancellation}\\
 \Downarrow\\
 \text{palindromicity}\ \Longleftrightarrow\ \text{transitivity}
 \ \Longleftrightarrow\ \text{gamma-positivity}.
 \end{gathered}
\]
A verification of the second chain would not by itself formalize the
preorder-polytope theorem: the bridge from lattice points to matching
supports must also be checked.

Manuscript~01 displays the central dependencies without any hidden appeal to
this report's unrefereed results:
\begin{center}
\begin{tikzpicture}[
 box/.style={draw=Accent,fill=Pale,rounded corners=1mm,
 align=center,font=\small,inner sep=6pt,text width=58mm},
 >={Stealth[length=2mm]}]
\node[box] (prior) at (0,0) {Published root-polytope identities\\
K\'alm\'an--Postnikov; Ohsugi--Tsuchiya};
\node[box] (bridge) at (0,-1.55) {Demand--support counting lemma\\
plus the explicit hypertree reduction};
\node[box] (zero) at (-3.7,-3.05) {Full zero-set Hall reduction};
\node[box] (cancel) at (3.7,-3.05) {Relation graph and\\overlap cancellation};
\node[box] (main) at (0,-4.6) {Universal multivariate expansion\\and nonnegative gamma coefficients};
\draw[->,Accent] (prior)--(bridge);
\draw[->,Accent] (bridge)--(zero);
\draw[->,Accent] (bridge)--(cancel);
\draw[->,Accent] (zero)--(main);
\draw[->,Accent] (cancel)--(main);
\end{tikzpicture}
\end{center}
The two lower branches are different reductions but share the same counting
bridge. The degree, deletion, monotonicity, and block laws then use only
finite combinatorics and polynomial algebra.

\subsection{Three easy ways to state a false variant (manuscript~01)}
\paragraph{Using only covering relations.}
For the chain $1<2<3$, the relation $1<3$ must appear in the matching graph.
Using just the two Hasse edges would give $\gamma_1=2$, whereas the correct
value is $3$ and $h=1+6t+6t^2+t^3$. The lower ideals are unchanged by taking
transitive closure, but the compatible-pair formula is not unchanged unless
the closure is taken as well. The second proof of
Corollary~\ref{gam:cor:cancel} explains why: paths must be compressible to
edges of the relation.

\paragraph{Testing only principal ideals.}
For the poset $a<b$ and $a<c$, the vector $(x_a,x_b,x_c)=(0,2,2)$ satisfies
the inequalities for the three principal ideals. It violates the inequality
for the full ideal, because its total is four while the ground set has size
three. The polytope in \eqref{gam:eq:Q} uses \emph{all} lower ideals.

\paragraph{Counting witnesses rather than supports.}
With two sources and two targets and every source below every target,
there are two compatible bijections but only one ordered support pair with
those specified sources and targets. That pair contributes one, not two.
This distinction is the reason the gamma polynomial is not in general the
ordinary matching generating polynomial.

\subsection{Real-rootedness and flagness remain separate}
The universal theorem does not imply real-rootedness. Part~II's height-two
analysis already contains counterexamples, with the earlier small example
credited to Shivam Patel~\cite{Patel}. To see the distinction in an exact
instance (manuscript~01), let $\Theta_3$ be the bipartite graph consisting
of three internally disjoint paths of length three with common endpoints.
Its height-two poset has gamma polynomial
\[
 p(u)=1+9u+24u^2+16u^3+u^4,
\]
as counted in Part~II (the case $m=3$ of~\eqref{htwo:eq:thetapoly}). The
identity
\[
 p(-x)=(1-x)(1-4x)^2+x^4
\]
shows that $p$ is positive on $[-1,0]$. Its nonnegative coefficients exclude
nonnegative roots. If all its roots were real, all four would therefore be
strictly below $-1$, contradicting the fact that their product is one.
A nonreal zero $u_0$ of $p$ lifts through $t=u_0(1+t)^2$ to nonreal
zeros of $h_\tau(t)=(1+t)^8p(t/(1+t)^2)$: the quotient is real for real
$t\ne-1$, and $t=-1$ cannot solve this equation. Thus $h_\tau$ is not
real-rooted. This example and its discovery are not claimed anew. [Merge
note: this is a short second proof that $P_{\Theta_3}$ is not real-rooted;
Part~II's Theorem~\ref{htwo:thm:thetaintro} counts its real roots exactly,
and Proposition~\ref{htwo:prop:roottransfer} is the lifting argument.]

Similarly, Boolean orbits of an auxiliary finite set are not a flag
simplicial sphere. Theorem~\ref{gam:gv:thm:orbits} supplies a nonnegative gamma
expansion, not the face-incidence structure demanded by
Conjecture~5.1(d). The root-polytope formula in
\eqref{gam:gv:eq:rootrecovered} does not remove that distinction.

\subsection{Suggested Lean development order (manuscript~06)}
A useful first module would treat finite relations, disjoint pairs of finite
sets, and relation-respecting bijections. It should prove the near-perfect
support/reachability lemma and the coefficient defect before introducing
any geometry. A second module would establish the ideal Hall criterion and
common-support cancellation, then perform the finite-sum reindexing that
gives the gamma expansion of $p_\tau$.

A third module can express the demand/support identity as an explicit
hypothesis and prove the support-fibre reduction and
Theorem~\ref{gam:thm:main} conditional on that hypothesis. Such a theorem must
be labeled conditional; it would not be a completed formal proof of the
conjecture. Discharging the hypothesis requires formalizing the classical
root-polytope route or developing a direct finite combinatorial proof.
The quotient-block and deficiency formulas can then be proved by finite
counting and elementary polynomial algebra.

The elementary graph classification is therefore a realistic independent
formalization target, while the main theorem has one substantial library
boundary. No Lean source or successful Lean compilation is claimed in this
package. Likewise, the external mathematical theorems used in the paper
are ordinary cited inputs, not assertions that they already exist in the
repository's trusted kernel boundary.

\subsection{A staged proof-assistant project (manuscript~01)}
A practical formalization can separate the new order-theoretic work from
the existing geometric input. First define finite preorders, lower ideals,
and the support-fiber excess map. The equivalence in
Lemma~\ref{gam:lem:fibre} is a finite-sum inequality argument and should be a
small, independent target. Next prove the Boolean summation: this gives a
formal implication from the explicit counting-bridge statement to the
universal gamma theorem.

A second finite target is overlap cancellation. One may formalize the
partial-permutation graph and its path decomposition, or iterate a
bijection from a source until it reaches a target. Finiteness and injectivity
exclude entry into a cycle. The resulting compatible core supports the
commuting toggles and every restriction-counting identity.

To obtain a fully closed formal proof, the counting bridge must itself be
proved rather than silently postulated. The supplied ordinary proof reduces
it to Hall's theorem and the two published root-polytope results; a natural
bijective proof of the bridge could yield a substantially smaller formal
library dependency. A Lean theorem with an unproved bridge hypothesis is a
valuable intermediate artifact, but is not a complete formal verification
of Conjecture~5.2. No uncompiled Lean file or such conditional proof is
presented as verified in this package.

[Merge note: neither plan has been started. No Lean or Rocq declaration in
ProveIt formalizes any statement of Part~IV.]

\section{Further research questions and topics}\label{gam:sec:questions}
The following questions are proposed research directions. Except where an
existing conjecture is named, no claim is made that the formulation is new
or that the literature has never addressed a related version (manuscript~06).
Manuscript~01 adds: the flag question is the remaining named conjecture in
the cited source; the others are proposed directions arising from the proof,
whose worldwide open status has not been exhaustively surveyed, and they
should be viewed as specific research tasks, not as assertions that the
literature contains no related results. Research
questions~\ref{gam:q:first}--\ref{gam:q:review} are manuscript~06's, in its
order, with manuscript~01's formulation added where it asks the same
question; Research questions~\ref{gam:gv:q:faces}--36 are the rest of
manuscript~01's.

\begin{question}[A direct demand/support bijection; manuscripts~06 and~01]\label{gam:q:first}\label{gam:q:bijection}
Construct an explicit bijection in Lemma~\ref{gam:lem:count} that is compatible
with right-support restriction as in Proposition~\ref{gam:prop:gradedinput}.
A mere lexicographic matching of two equally sized lists is not the target.
Can an augmenting-path or activity construction explain the correspondence
without importing Ehrhart theory? This would remove the largest
formalization dependency of the main theorem. (Manuscript~01: construct an
explicit bijection from $\mathcal R_H$ to perfectly matchable support pairs
that preserves the exact receiver support. Can it respect induced subgraphs
or a controlled choice of vertex ordering?)
\end{question}
Proposition~\ref{gam:prop:gradedinput} proves the required cardinality
identities, but the geometric proof does not identify a natural map. Such a
construction would make the Boolean action on actual preorder lattice points
explicit and would simplify a complete formalization (manuscript~01).
[Merge note: this is Part~II's Research question~\ref{gam:q:bridge}, asked
again with the receiver support preserved.]

\begin{question}[Boolean orbits on actual lattice points]\label{gam:q:orbits}
Can one realize the terms in \eqref{gam:eq:main} as orbits of a natural system
of involutions on $\Qt\cap\Z^E$, with a $k$th orbit containing one point
for every subset of $n-2k$ free labels? Compatibility with relabeling and
restriction is desirable. The present support-count identity does not
supply such an action.
\end{question}
[Merge note: manuscript~01's Theorem~\ref{gam:gv:thm:orbits} realizes such
orbits on the support-pair model $\mathscr M_\tau$, not on $\Qt\cap\Z^E$;
the question stays open as stated.]

\begin{question}[Flag-polytopal realization; manuscripts~06 and~01]\label{gam:q:flag}
Does every $h_\tau$ occur as the $h$-polynomial of an $n$-dimensional flag
simplicial polytope, as asked in \cite[Conjecture~5.1(d)]{AC}? The gamma
formula gives nonnegative coefficients but no flag face complex. A useful
intermediate objective is a simplicial model whose face numbers are the
transport-support counts; it must account for the fact that deleting an
arbitrary single signed endpoint need not preserve balance. (Manuscript~01:
can the disjoint-support model be used to construct a flag simplicial
$n$-polytope whose $h$-polynomial is $h_\tau$?)
\end{question}
The existing Boolean families provide ranks but no face-gluing rules.
A successful construction must address intersections and sphere topology,
not merely reproduce the gamma coefficients. It need not use the same
canonical lattice triangulation considered in Part~I (manuscript~01).
[Merge note: compare Part~II's Research questions~6 and~7 and its crown
obstruction, Theorem~\ref{htwo:thm:crownintro}.]

\begin{question}[Classification beyond a perfect matching]
Extend Corollary~\ref{gam:cor:balanced} to arbitrary bipartite graphs, including
unequal shores and graphs whose maximum matching does not saturate either
shore. Is there a useful decomposition of the palindromic cases into
balanced preorder blocks and residual components? Palindromicity is to be
measured in the actual polynomial degree. The endpoint argument here does
not directly apply when that degree is smaller than a shore size.
\end{question}

\begin{question}[Coefficient shape beyond gamma-positivity]
Which preorders have log-concave, unimodal, or real-rooted gamma polynomials?
The general theorem answers none of these stronger questions for
$\Gamma_\tau$. For real-rootedness, the already documented height-two
counterexamples rule out an unrestricted assertion. Relations to quotient
width and induced comparability configurations are concrete targets.
\end{question}

\begin{question}[Extremal profiles with prescribed structure]
Determine the maximal and minimal $\gamma_k$ when block sizes, quotient
width, or matching number are fixed. Theorem~\ref{gam:thm:bounds} gives sharp
unrestricted bounds, but fixing these structural parameters may make the
extremizers quite different from chains or universal equivalence classes.
The positive block formula offers a way to separate local multiplicities
from quotient feasibility.
\end{question}

\begin{question}[Parameterized exact algorithms]
Can \eqref{gam:eq:signed} be evaluated efficiently for bounded-width quotient
posets, or when the \emph{comparability graph} has bounded treewidth? A
cover-graph parameter is a separate question because transitive closure
can enlarge neighborhoods and graph width. Establish precise arithmetic
and bit-complexity bounds, and determine the counting complexity without
parameter restrictions.
\end{question}

\begin{question}[A geometric interpretation of the gamma coefficients]
The signed-vector formula counts balanced points of the cube
$\{-1,0,1\}^E$ satisfying ideal inequalities. Can these points be realized
as a natural collection of faces, lattice points in a canonical section,
or standard monomials of a graded algebra? Such an interpretation might
connect positivity to Part~I's root-polytope model, but would
require more than the enumerative identity established here.
\end{question}

\begin{question}[Limit laws for support size]
For growing families of preorders, what conditions on $\Gamma_\tau$ or the
quotient structure imply a central limit theorem for $L$? The mixture in
Theorem~\ref{gam:thm:statistics} reduces the question to the fluctuations of its
mixing variable and the conditional binomial size. Can the variance and
tail bounds be sharpened using comparability density or matching
structure, rather than $n$ alone?
\end{question}

\begin{question}[Independent formal and mathematical review; manuscripts~06 and~01]\label{gam:q:review}
Formalize first the elementary endpoint-defect and cancellation theorems,
and independently review the support-fibre equivalence. Then discharge the
bipartite counting bridge in a proof assistant. In parallel, compare the
balanced-graph characterization with existing Gorenstein and
matching-decomposition criteria to determine which parts are already
implicit in the literature and sharpen the priority assessment.
(Manuscript~01: formalize the universal theorem with no unproved
assumptions, and develop an independent proof of the counting bridge that
avoids the root-polytope machinery.)
\end{question}
The finite-order part is short enough to audit separately. Closing the
bridge dependency, while retaining the multivariate support interpretation,
would turn the current mathematical proof into a compact and reusable
formal combinatorial library result (manuscript~01).

\begin{question}[Which gamma vectors are face vectors?; manuscript~01]\label{gam:gv:q:faces}
Do the numbers $\gamma_k(\tau)$ arise as the face numbers of a useful simplicial
or balanced complex, with a construction natural under induced restriction?
\end{question}
Simply treating compatible pairs as faces is inadequate: removing one
source without a receiver destroys equal cardinality. The locality formula
\eqref{gam:gv:eq:deckgamma} suggests studying endpoint-supported structures or
quotients of matching complexes with carefully controlled identifications.

\begin{question}[The even-cardinality reconstruction defect; manuscript~01]
What additional deletion, marking, or contraction data determines the top
gamma coefficient when $n$ is even? Can one reconstruct it from a suitably
enriched polynomial deck without reconstructing the entire preorder?
\end{question}
Equation~\eqref{gam:gv:eq:deletiondifferential} reconstructs every other
coefficient but annihilates the middle monomial.
Corollary~\ref{gam:gv:cor:derivative} identifies the missing scalar as a
signed value at $-1$, counted by full endpoint cores. This gives a precise
target for richer reconstruction data.

\begin{question}[Exact counting complexity; manuscript~01]
For which classes of quotient posets can the profile formula be evaluated
more efficiently than $O(n^{2b}2^b)$? What is the complexity of computing a
specified $\gamma_k$, or the whole gamma polynomial, for unrestricted preorders?
\end{question}
A single pair's feasibility is a matching problem; counting distinct pairs
is a different task. The fixed-block algorithm gives one tractable regime,
but does not supply a general efficient counting algorithm or a hardness
classification. Tree decompositions of the quotient and repeated
neighborhood classes are natural structural parameters.

\begin{question}[Local changes of the relation; manuscript~01]
Find effective formulas for the gamma increment when one adds a relation
and then takes transitive closure. Can the newly compatible cores be
partitioned without overcounting multiple new witnessing matchings?
\end{question}
Theorem~\ref{gam:thm:monotone} already proves nonnegativity of every increment.
An exact local rule would be stronger and could lead to constructive
recurrences, particularly for modular or series--parallel quotient
operations. The closure operation must be included in the input model.

\begin{question}[Root geometry and negative dependence; manuscript~01]
Identify structural classes of preorders for which $\Gamma_\tau$ is
real-rooted or admits a useful multivariate stability refinement. Which
operations preserve these properties in the presence of nontrivial blocks?
\end{question}
General positivity is now separated from these stronger analytic properties.
The arbitrary height-two bipartite subclass already contains nonreal-root
examples, so bounded height alone is insufficient. Part~III's cactus
results offer positive comparison cases, not a classification of all
preorders. [Merge note: compare Part~II's Research question~3 and Part~III's
Research question~11.]

\begin{question}[Refining the lattice-point statistic; manuscript~01]
What counterpart of the Boolean expansion survives if one records both
support and coordinate sum, or weights the individual excess coordinates?
\end{question}
The present proof deliberately forgets the total demand in the classical
bridge. A two-parameter identity would require extra information about its
hypertree or matching-support correspondence. It cannot be obtained by
inserting arbitrary weights into an equality of unweighted cardinalities.

\begin{question}[Beyond unit ideal budgets; manuscript~01]\label{gam:gv:q:last}
Which integral budget functions $b(I)$ admit an analogue for
$\{x\ge0:x(I)\le b(I)\}$? What replaces the centered gamma basis when the
all-one vector is no longer the unique full-support lattice point?
\end{question}
The excess substitution changes the right-hand side to
$b(I)-|I\cap S|$. The special cancellation into the number of zero
coordinates is lost. Any extension must identify new supply capacities or
a different symmetry center, rather than assuming the same formula persists.

\section{Conclusion}\label{gam:sec:conclusion}
The main formula treats the gamma coefficients of a preorder not as
mysterious alternating combinations of lattice-point counts, but as counts
of disjoint feasible transport supports. Fixing a lattice point's zeros
exposes a bipartite demand problem; summing those problems exposes Boolean
intervals. This proves gamma-positivity for every finite preorder, including
all equivalence-class blowups and all heights.

The matching viewpoint also gives a structural converse. In a balanced
bipartite graph with a perfect matching, a palindromic support polynomial
forces the relation obtained from that matching to be transitive. The
failure is measured exactly by missing reachability pairs. Positive block
formulas, coefficientwise monotonicity, sharp bounds, and the exact root
multiplicity at $-1$ then follow from the same finite support model.

The unresolved step toward a more conceptual and more readily formalized
proof is not another positivity inequality. It is a natural combinatorial
realization of the inherited demand/support equinumeracy. The proofs and
checks supplied here isolate that issue while giving an explicit answer to
the stated gamma-positivity conjecture.

Manuscript~01 concludes in the same sense. The general preorder problem
admits a uniform solution once zero coordinates are allowed on the entire
ground set. The ideal inequalities then become a bipartite supply--demand
system, and the classical counting bridge turns each support fiber into a
family of matchable pairs. Summing over the free coordinates produces the
gamma basis with no cancellation of signs. The resulting coefficients count
ordered disjoint compatible source and receiver sets. This proves the full
gamma-positivity conjecture, explains why arbitrary height and arbitrary
equivalence blocks cause no exception, and supplies an exact
matching-theoretic meaning for the vanishing at $-1$. The same finite model
gives relation-monotonicity, complement-duality, induced-deck identities,
and a block-capacity formula. The remaining work is not to infer positivity
from more examples, but to understand natural bijections, geometric
realizations, stronger root properties, and complete formal verification of
the classical bridge and its new consequences.

\section{Normalization and independent-review checklist}\label{gam:sec:normalization}
\noindent\emph{(Appendix~A of manuscript~06.)}

\subsection{The three different cardinalities}
A perfect matching is a set of edges. A matchable support pair $(U,V)$ is a
pair of vertex sets admitting at least one perfect matching. A hypertree is
a degree-minus-one vector admitting at least one realizing spanning tree.
The proofs count the latter two without realization multiplicity. The
$K_{2,2}$ example in Remark~\ref{gam:rem:K22} is a minimal check against
conflating these notions.

\subsection{Three polynomial models, with different dimensions}
The original preorder polytope $\Qt$ lies in $\R^n$, and $h_\tau$ counts
its points by support. The doubled graph $G_\tau$ has $2n$ vertices and
$p_{G_\tau}$ has degree $n$. The augmented edge polytope
$B_{\widehat{G_\tau}}$ has dimension $2n$ and its Ehrhart numerator is the
same polynomial, with higher coefficients zero. These dimension statements
are consistent because the degree of an Ehrhart numerator can be strictly
smaller than the polytope dimension.

For a general graph $H$ with shores of sizes $p,q$, its augmented graph has
$p+q+2$ vertices, is connected and bipartite, and its edge polytope has
dimension $p+q$. The two affine equations say that the coordinate sum on
each augmented shore is one. The natural affine lattice is the intersection
of that affine space with the ambient integer lattice. No factor of a
lattice index is inserted in \eqref{htwo:eq:KPinput}.

\subsection{Orientation and equality cases}
A relation edge is always directed from a source in the lower direction to
a target in the upper direction. Hence a matching must satisfy
$|A\cap I|\ge|B\cap I|$ for ideals $I$, not the reverse inequality. In the
near-perfect-support lemma, omitting $a$ on the source side and $b$ on the
target side detects a path from $b$ to $a$. The count $|\overline R|$ is
invariant under swapping their dummy names, but the proof is not.

The signed-vector formula uses signs on individual elements, not just
blocks. Once block differences are fixed, the multinomial coefficients in
\eqref{gam:eq:localB} recover precisely the lost labeling information. The
formula would be wrong without these factors.

\subsection{A short audit path}
An independent reviewer can check the main theorem by verifying the two
implications of Lemma~\ref{gam:lem:fibre}, the normalization in the established
input Lemma~\ref{gam:lem:count}, and the finite sum in
\eqref{gam:eq:fixedsupport}. The second principal theorem can be checked
separately from the directed-path construction in Lemma~\ref{gam:lem:reach} and
the cancellation counterexample in Proposition~\ref{gam:prop:canceliff}.
None of those steps depends on the finite verification data.

\section{A compact proof certificate}\label{gam:gv:sec:certificate}
\noindent\emph{(Appendix~A of manuscript~01.)}
This section records the complete logical skeleton for a reader checking
the generalization rather than its consequences.

Let $S\subseteq E$, $Z=E\setminus S$, and $H_{Z,S}$ have edge $zs$ when
$z\le_\tau s$. Write $x=\one_S+c$ on $S$, with zero coordinates on $Z$.
For $T\subseteq S$,
\[
 x\in\Qt
 \ \Longrightarrow\
 c(T)\le c(S\cap\down T)
 \le |Z\cap\down T|=|N_{H_{Z,S}}(T)|.
\]
Conversely, for a lower ideal $I$,
\[
 c(I\cap S)\le |Z\cap\down(I\cap S)|\le |Z\cap I|
 \ \Longrightarrow\ x(I)\le|I|.
\]
Thus the exact-support fiber has size $|\mathcal R_{H_{Z,S}}|$.
The classical bridge says that this is
\[
 \#\{(U,V):U\subseteq Z,\ V\subseteq S,\ U\match_\tau V\}.
\]
For any fixed disjoint compatible $(U,V)$, its allowed supports are exactly
$S=V\sqcup C$, $C\subseteq E\setminus(U\cup V)$. Hence
\[
 H_\tau(\zv)
 =\sum_{(U,V)}\sum_C z_{V\cup C}
 =\sum_{(U,V)}z_V\prod_{w\notin U\cup V}(1+z_w).
\]
This proves the theorem. The only imported count is justified by the
explicit demand-to-hypertree-vector correspondence and the published
identities \eqref{htwo:eq:KPinput}--\eqref{htwo:eq:OTinput}. No statement about
preorder gamma-positivity is used in those inputs.

\phantomsection\addcontentsline{toc}{part}{Appendices to Part I}
\section{A hypertree proof of the known root identity}\label{app:hypertrees}

This appendix reconstructs Proposition~\ref{prop:known} without using the
support-enumerator theorem of~\cite{DHLTW}. It is included for conceptual
clarity and as an independent route to the same normalization. Its one
substantial imported result is the K\'alm\'an--Postnikov theorem~\cite{KP}.

\subsection{Hypertrees and internal inactivity}

For a connected bipartite graph with left shore $L$, a left hypertree is a
vector $a\in\N^L$ realized by a spanning tree $T$ through
$a_i=\deg_T(i_L)-1$. Distinct trees realizing the same vector contribute only
one hypertree. Order the left shore. A coordinate $i$ is internally inactive
when there is a smaller coordinate $j$ such that replacing $a$ by
$a-\ee_i+\ee_j$ gives another hypertree. The interior polynomial is the sum
of $t^{\text{number of internally inactive coordinates}}$ over hypertrees.

The K\'alm\'an--Postnikov theorem identifies this interior polynomial with the
Ehrhart numerator of the bipartite root polytope. Their paper uses a reversed
convention for the triangulation $h$-polynomial in its Theorem~1.1; the
Ehrhart-basis formulation immediately following that theorem, equation (1.2),
identifies the coefficients with the usual $h^*$-numerator used here. Thus
there is no additional reversal of $t$ in the statement we apply.

\begin{lemma}\label{lem:hypertrees}
The left hypertrees of $\widehat G_\tau$ are exactly
\begin{equation}
 a=(n-x(E),x_1,\ldots,x_n),\qquad x\in P_\tau.\label{eq:hypertree}
\end{equation}
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
A spanning tree of $\widehat G_\tau$ has $2n+1$ edges, so the sum of its left
degree-minus-one vector is $(2n+1)-(n+1)=n$. For a nonempty
$A\subseteq[n]$, the tree edges incident with $A_L$ form a forest on
$A_L\cup N(A)$. Their number is $a(A)+|A|$, and a forest on this nonempty
vertex set has at most $|A|+|N(A)|-1$ edges. Hence
\[
 a(A)\leq |N(A)|-1=|\down A|.
\]
Lemma~\ref{lem:draconian} now shows that $x=(a_1,\ldots,a_n)$ belongs to
$P_\tau$, and the sum condition determines $a_0$.

Conversely, let $x\in P_\tau$. Replace the left element $i$ by $x_i$ distinct
clones, each adjacent to the right set $\down i\subseteq[n]$. A set of clones
whose labels lie in $A$ has at most $x(A)\leq|\down A|$ members. Thus Hall's
matching condition holds, and all clones can be matched to distinct right
vertices. This is also a constructive statement: if an augmenting-path
matching leaves a clone unmatched, the alternating paths reachable from it
produce a set of clones with fewer neighbors, contradicting Hall's condition.

Build a tree by first taking all edges $i_L0_R$ for $0\leq i\leq n$. Each
right vertex $j_R$, $j>0$, is then attached to the left label of its matched
clone, or to $0_L$ if it is unmatched. The first edges form a star; every
subsequent edge attaches a new leaf. Hence the result is a spanning tree.
Its left degrees are $x_i+1$ for $i>0$ and $n-x(E)+1$ for $i=0$, exactly
as required.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}\label{lem:activity}
Order the left vertices with $0$ first. For the hypertree in
\eqref{eq:hypertree}, internal inactivity is $|\supp(x)|$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
The first coordinate is never internally inactive. A zero coordinate cannot
transfer a unit, so it is also not internally inactive. If $x_i>0$, then
$x-\ee_i\in P_\tau$ because all the defining inequalities are upper bounds
and the coordinates remain nonnegative. By Lemma~\ref{lem:hypertrees}, the
vector obtained by transferring one unit from coordinate $i$ to coordinate
$0$ is another hypertree. Thus every positive nonzero-index coordinate is
internally inactive, and there are no others.
\end{proof}

Summing over~\eqref{eq:hypertree} gives an interior polynomial equal to
$h_\tau(t)$. The K\'alm\'an--Postnikov theorem identifies it with
$h^*(B_\tau,t)$, proving Proposition~\ref{prop:known} by this second route.
Notice that the argument counts hypertree \emph{vectors}, not spanning trees;
counting all realizing trees would introduce incorrect multiplicities.

\section{Proof dependencies and normalization audit}\label{app:audit}

\begin{center}
\begin{tabularx}{\textwidth}{@{}p{0.29\textwidth}Y@{}}
\toprule
Claim or input & Where it comes from\\
\midrule
$h^*(B_\tau)=h_\tau$ & Known: \cite[Theorem~1.2]{DHLTW}, specialized using
Lemma~\ref{lem:draconian}. Alternatively Appendix~\ref{app:hypertrees} plus
\cite{KP}.\\[3pt]
Ideal Hall presentation & Proposition~\ref{prop:inequalities}; transitivity is
used explicitly.\\[3pt]
Integer fiber factorization & Theorem~\ref{thm:fibers}; all coordinates and
inverse parameters are displayed.\\[3pt]
$h^*(C_\tau)=h_\tau$ & Corollary~\ref{cor:numerators}; a consequence of the
fiber factorization and the known root identity.\\[3pt]
Unimodularity & Leaf elimination in Lemma~\ref{lem:determinant}, followed by
Proposition~\ref{prop:startriangulation}.\\[3pt]
Polytopal pulling boundary & The established pulling lemma
\cite[Lemma~2.1]{AthSpecial}.\\[3pt]
Palindromicity, unimodality & Dehn--Sommerville and the $g$-theorem applied to
the constructed simplicial polytope; an independent reciprocity proof of
palindromicity is in Section~\ref{sec:second}.\\[3pt]
Alternative polytopal route & The explicit special simplex and
\cite[Theorem~3.5]{AthSpecial}.\\
\bottomrule
\end{tabularx}
\end{center}

There are several easy places to make an otherwise plausible argument wrong.
The support polynomial of $Q_\tau$ is not asserted to be $h^*(Q_\tau)$.
It is the numerator of the different polytopes $B_\tau$ and $C_\tau$.
The larger denominator has exponent $2n+1$ and the smaller one exponent $n+1$.
Both auxiliary vertices, and the edge between them, are required. The edge
orientation convention is $j\leq_\tau i\iff i_Lj_R$ is present. The
integer-fiber parameters consist of $n+1$ nonnegative entries; the exponent
$n$ in~\eqref{eq:seriesfactor} results only after dividing by the additional
slack factor in the Ehrhart series of $C_\tau$.

No argument substitutes a rational equivalence for an integral one. The
fiber base is integral because $a_\tau(y)$ is an integer for integral $y$.
The determinant statements refer to the full relevant lattices. Finally,
regularity or shellability alone is not used to claim that a sphere is
polytopal: the specific pulling-boundary theorem supplies that step.

\section{Reproducing the accompanying computations}\label{app:reproduce}

The archive contains \texttt{article.tex}, \texttt{article.pdf}, a build script,
\texttt{code/verify.py}, the full CSV enumeration, a JSON result report, and
status notes. From the extracted directory, run
\begin{lstlisting}
python3 code/verify.py --output data
pdflatex -interaction=nonstopmode -halt-on-error article.tex
pdflatex -interaction=nonstopmode -halt-on-error article.tex
\end{lstlisting}
The verifier requires Python 3.10 or later and no third-party Python packages.
Do not use Python's \texttt{-O} option, since assertions are part of the checks.
The LaTeX document uses standard TeX Live packages, including
\texttt{newpxtext}, \texttt{newpxmath}, \texttt{tcolorbox}, and \texttt{hyperref}.

The CSV uses zero-based bit positions: the row value $5$ represents the set
$\{0,2\}$. Row $i$ records the principal ideal of element $i$, not its filter.
A polynomial is stored as coefficients from degree zero upward. The JSON
report also records explicit edge sets for matching certificates and complete
Ehrhart counts for the independently tested instances.

[Added 28 September 2026, batch 39: this appendix describes Part~I's
package; Part~II's programs and how to rerun them are in
Section~\ref{htwo:sec:build}.]

The calculations in the supplied report completed without failed assertions.
Their runtime is recorded separately as an observation from this environment,
not as a performance guarantee. No claim of formal verification is made.
